# OpenID Federation conformance matrix (Library)

This matrix maps every normative statement (MUST, SHOULD, MAY and their variants) of three specifications to the
Library source:

- [OpenID Federation 1.1 Final](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-1_1-final.html) (5 May 2026)
- [OpenID Federation for OpenID Connect 1.1 Final](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-connect-1_1-final.html) (5 May 2026)
- [OpenID Federation Wallet Architectures 1.0 draft 05](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-wallet-1_0-05.html) (15 February 2026)

The statements were extracted mechanically from the published HTML, one row per sentence that carries a keyword. A
sentence with two keywords is one row, and a claim definition such as "OPTIONAL. ..." is a row as well. The ID links to
the section in the published specification. Code and test references point at `develop` as of 2026-10-18 (commit b690184);
line numbers drift as the code changes, so treat them as a starting point.

The [OpenID Federation 1.1 library guide](OPENID-FEDERATION-1.1.md) explains the features this matrix refers to. The
enterprise stack built on this Library has its own matrix in the Enterprise Development Kit documentation.

## How to read it

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| implemented | The Library enforces or produces the required behaviour on the paths that apply. |
| partial | Some paths or cases do it and others do not, or a check exists but was not verified for every case. The note says which. |
| missing | The Library plays the role the statement addresses and does not do it. For a MAY this means the optional feature is not offered. |
| n.a. | The statement addresses a role the Library does not play, is a choice for a federation operator or profile, is a definition with nothing to implement, or is IANA registration text. |

What this matrix is and is not:

- It is a source review. A row marked implemented means the code does what the statement asks; it is not a test
  result. Tests are listed where one exercises the behaviour.
- Conformance and interop runs are evidence for the behaviour they exercised, not certification. The Library has not
  been certified by the OpenID Foundation.
- Wallet rows describe what the wallet module validates. Wallet metadata checks are not wallet protocol conformance.
- The Library is a set of service commands. Several obligations belong to the host that uses them: who may call a
  command, which Trust Anchors are accepted, how an RP builds its authentication request. The notes say where the
  Library leaves a step to the host.

## Live evidence

The Testcontainers interop harness (`modules/openid-federation-interop-tests`) runs the Library servers against go-oidfed
LightHouse and OFCLI. It covers chain resolution in both directions, metadata policy across mixed chains, naming and
path-length constraints, Trust Mark issuance, filtering and revocation, ES256, ES384, ES512 and RS256 Entity
Configurations accepted by LightHouse, and key rollover. The last clean run passed 14 of 15 scenarios. The failure was
on the peer side: LightHouse keeps signing with its previous key after a rotation. These runs predate the fixes
listed above, and they are interop evidence for the scenarios they cover, not conformance results.

| [WAL-039](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-wallet-1_0-05.html#section-8.2) | 8.2 | SHOULD | Wallets SHOULD remain neutral in attesting to the reliability of their Wallet Providers for the End-User, thereby protecting the End-User against any malevolent behavior by the Wallet Provider. | n.a. |  |  | Wallet UX neutrality guidance; nothing to implement in a library. |
